The 300-frame memory line counts these entries (`evicted with pools N (serial entries that went with them M)`). Left as before: a ghost entry that holds a package's texture after the package itself has gone (no table names it any more); the finalized-texture table's drain lets it go once it idles above the table's floor.

  A resolution racing the Finalize keeps nothing either (NX 18's own; upstream's port has the race). `nvnMemoryPoolFinalize` clears the pool's magic first, and `mtl_alias_drop_pool` marks the pool's texture IDs gone and ends its textures' entries in one hold of `g_rmu` (taken before `g_smu`). With the entries ended under `g_smu` alone and the IDs marked in a later hold, a walk in between (`pool_gpu`, holding `g_rmu`) found an ID not gone and the magic still set, and `tex_mtl_`, finding no entry, made the texture again: a live entry for the serial, which had left the pool index, and a sharing-table entry under the dead pool's key, kept until the object's own Finalize, the hold the port removes (Ocarina of Time's shadow target, if the queue worker walked its IDs during that Finalize). Marking the IDs first in a hold of their own would not do: a binding in between finds the entry still there through `tex_made` and caches a view of it (`vc_put`), which keeps the texture as long. A texture object resolved outside `g_rmu` after the magic is cleared takes the entry it has (`tex_made`). One that read the magic just before keeps nothing either, whatever has become of its serial meanwhile: a resolution reads the count of Finalizes before the pool's magic (`fin_snap`), the Finalize counts itself in its hold of `g_smu` once the pool's CPU-written classification has gone and records the count under the pool's address (`pool_fin_note`, `g_pool_fin`), and each put of the resolution, under `g_smu`, is refused when its pool was finalized since its reading (`pool_fin_since`): no entry under the serial, live or finalized, none in the sharing table and none in the packaged-texture table. The Finalize also marks its live serials (`g_pool_ended`, until the object's own Finalize or initialization again), which refuses any later resolution of them. The mark alone (`5f7ee174`) missed a serial finalized before its pool, the usual NVN teardown order (Breath of the Wild finalizes texture objects before it submits), and one whose object's own Finalize came between the pool's and the put: such a resolution put a finalized-texture entry and a sharing-table entry under the dead pool's key, held until a later pool at that address was finalized and handed meanwhile to a new pool's texture there of the same offset and description. Ocarina of Time's shadow target is not one (its object outlives its pool). A package's serial entry and packaged-texture entry go in one hold, both or neither (`tex_mtl_`), the texture upgrade reads the magic itself (`tex_upgrade`: a pinned texture whose pool is gone is upgraded for its use alone), and the drop of a texture taken from its storage reads the count before the records (`cpu_unc_drop`), so a pinned package's texture is not handed to its serial after the Finalize. The table keeps the newest 2,048 addresses once it passes 4,096 (`POOL_FIN_KEEP`); a reading older than those let go counts as finalized since. What such a resolution made serves its use and is retired after the submission in flight (`tex_put_made`), and a part of a package handed over (`cpu_unc_drop`) is left to the package. Each refusal logs (`resolved as its memory pool's Finalize ended it: not kept`, the first four).
- **Check:** `t_pool_finalize` (NX 18's, upstream's `t_serial_owner` cases on this tree's tables: the pool's live objects lose their entries, and so do a finalized serial's entry and other live serials' entries that name one of its shared textures or a view of one, and a live object's entry holding a package, a finalized serial's entry holding the package's texture and one holding a package of the pool that no live object holds, each noted; another pool's textures keep theirs; a pin still holds its texture; the objects' later Finalize ends nothing; a pressure pass of `DM_NVN_RT_DEAD` and the Finalize each release a target once, and a pool finalized first leaves the next pass nothing; a walk holding `g_rmu` while another thread finalizes the pool resolves the texture it had, and the Finalize still holds `g_rmu` as the entries end; a resolution that read the magic before the Finalize, a package made then and a package's texture handed over keep no entry, the pinned package keeping its texture resident; resolutions that read the pool as live just before a game thread's Finalize, the Finalize running there (a test hook, `g_pool_read_after`), keep no entry whether the object lives on, was finalized before its pool or between: a target in a recycled heap resolved off the submit thread, an allocation of its own, a buffer texture and a package, each kept until the submission in flight completes (the heap not handed out before) and then freed; a pinned sRGB view upgraded after its pool's Finalize keeps no entry and its new parent is freed; the finalized pools' table stays bounded; the pool index is checked against the live texture objects after every step; with the port's order 3 of its 111 checks fail, with the IDs marked first 1, without `g_pool_ended` 9; of the 141 checks now, without the finalize count 10 fail, with it read after the magic 6, a made texture not retired 3, a buffer texture put without its retirement 1, the upgrade's view not retired 1), `t_cpu_tex --metal` (`finalize_during_walk`: with the real `nvnMemoryPoolFinalize`, the magic is cleared and no entry ended while a walk holds `g_rmu`, and the walk's `tex_mtl` takes the texture the entry has; `handover_during_finalize`: a GPU write's hand-over met by the Finalize hands a pinned package's texture to no serial, 2 checks fail with a plain put), `t_depth_lazy` (a whole clear met by the Finalize makes the normal texture for its use, keeps no entry and retires it, its object live or finalized between; 3 checks fail with a plain put), `t_rt_recycle` (an 8 MB target whose pool is finalized while its texture object is live is freed, its heap waits in the recycled list, a lookup makes nothing), `t_readback` (`stale_pools`: a live packaged or sparse texture of a finalized pool reads as an empty slot and nothing is uploaded).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (upstream [PR #2775](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2775), which found the holder with a trace of every texture of 256 MB or more). Upstream, on the Mac with the iPhone's settings, Kakariko Village to Hyrule Field and back from the owner's 2026-11-13 save (2026-11-15, the PR's version of the fix, which treats this shared target the same way): the old heap was let go about 330 submissions after the pool's Finalize, the set in the field was 3,850 MB against 4,211 MB, and the peak after a commit at the village load 4,142 MB against 4,375 MB. Whether long runs on the owner's iPhone 18 Pro Max keep less memory is **open**: neither NX 18 nor the phone has run the fix.

### Repeated GPU out-of-memory failures end the game

- **Symptom:** the Mac grows unresponsive for hours, then panics, with the host still submitting work that fails.
- **Cause:** a command buffer that fails with `Insufficient Memory` frees nothing, and the game submits the next one.
- **Fix:** general (`nvn_mtl.m` `res_top_once`): 32 such failures within 60 s end the process with a red line, as the session cannot recover and the machine may not either.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### A clear pass on a large depth texture fails with `Insufficient Memory`

- **Symptom:** a scene load logs `upload command buffer execution error: … Insufficient Memory (00000008:kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory)` right after a `large texture … format 0x32 … 296 MB` line, then `upload command buffer dropped`, then the game's own command buffers fail one after another, up to the 32-failures stop ([Repeated GPU out-of-memory failures end the game](#repeated-gpu-out-of-memory-failures-end-the-game)), with most of the GPU's working set free.
- **Cause:** a new texture is zeroed in the upload batch ([New textures start as zero](rendering.md#new-textures-start-as-zero)), and a depth one was zeroed by a clear-only render pass. On an M3 Pro (18 GB, macOS 27.3) such a pass on a large depth attachment ends in `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory` whatever memory is free, and the queue then fails every command buffer, trivial ones included, until the texture is freed. A standalone Metal 4 program failed for Depth32Float 4096x4096 and Depth16Unorm from about 5000x5000 up; copying zeros into the same textures, up to 16384x16384, never failed. Not seen on an M4 Pro (48 GB), where Ocarina of Time loads the same textures. Apple's driver is the cause; the host only chooses how it zeroes.
- **Fix:** the profile key `depth_zero_copy on` (Ocarina of Time; `DM_DEPTH_ZERO_COPY=1` / `0` overrides it; `nvn_mtl.m` `tex_zero_fill`; port of NXNative 4cdd5577, which does it for every game). Every single-sample Depth16Unorm or Depth32Float texture, of any size, is zeroed by buffer copies from the shared zero buffer, in pieces of rows that fit it (a row is at most 64 KB, the buffer at least 4 MB, so a depth texture never grows it), in the upload batch as before. Stencil formats and multisample textures keep the clear pass: a plain buffer copy cannot write them. Every other game keeps the clear pass while X1.0 is frozen: the copies write the same zeros, but they change the upload batch of every game with depth targets, and only Ocarina of Time's loads have been looked at.
- **Log:** with the key on, or the switch set either way, `new-texture zeroing: single-sample Depth16 / Depth32F textures by copies from the zero buffer (game profile depth_zero_copy)` (or `by a clear pass (DM_DEPTH_ZERO_COPY)`) at the first depth texture, and for each depth texture of 64 MB or more, once the GPU times are known (12 frames on, `dz_tick` from `mtl_set_frame`, which the replay worker calls: no line with queue replay off), `depth zero: the 12288x12288 Depth16Unorm (296 MB) made in frame F, zeroed by N copies (game profile depth_zero_copy): the upload batch with them … ms on the GPU; that frame's submissions … ms on the GPU, … ms and … ms the next two`. The upload batch's time (its feedback handler, `up_flush`) holds the copies or the clear pass with whatever else that batch uploads; the frames' times are the replay's own command buffers (`mtl_gpu_ms`), which do not count the upload batch. Up to 4 notes wait at once (`DZ_N`; a save load makes the 4096x4096 Depth32F of 64 MB too), a larger texture taking the smallest one's place when all 4 wait. N is the pieces of rows that fit the zero buffer at the time: 73 for the 12288x12288 with 4 MB, fewer once colour textures have grown the buffer (19 at 16 MB). The owner's iPhone 18 Pro Max logs (X1.0, the clear pass) show a GPU frame of 145-158 ms after each 12288x12288 texture (`[longframe] … GPU 149.0 ms` right after the `large texture` line); an A/B of `DM_DEPTH_ZERO_COPY=0` against the default tells from the batch's time whether that is the zeroing, and from the frames' times whether the frame changes with it (the texture's first residency or the game's own work if it does not).
- **Where it does not help:** a game that renders into a depth attachment this large: the same standalone program fails a load-and-draw pass into 12288x12288, scissored or not. Ocarina of Time only samples its 12288x12288 texture.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_zero_depth` (Depth16 and Depth32F from 17x5 to 16384x129, with levels, layers, a cube and a 64 MB 4096x4096, poisoned and read back as zero on the GPU, and the number of copies a level takes; under the Metal validation layer), with `depth_zero_copy` on and then off (no copies: the clear pass), the profile's default and `DM_DEPTH_ZERO_COPY=0` / `1`, the note a 64 MB texture leaves with its upload batch's GPU time from the batch's feedback, the `depth zero:` line's text, a save load's two textures in one frame giving a line each, and with 4 notes waiting a larger texture replacing the smallest, written once their frames' GPU times are due.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (NXNative PR #2306: an M3 Pro, where about half the save loads ended at the 32-failures stop; 203 runs on its build with copies for depth textures of 16 MB and more, and a retry throttle not landed upstream or here, none failed). Not yet run on the phone with the copies.

### Large depth textures that are only sampled take their whole size

- **Symptom:** a `large texture … format 0x32 … 296 MB` line (Depth16, 12288x12288) or a 64 MB Depth32F 4096x4096 at a scene or save load, then GPU memory and the footprint up by that much although the texture is never drawn into where the game is; on an iPhone a GPU frame of 145-158 ms after each one (Ocarina of Time, X1.0).
- **Cause:** a Metal texture is backed in full when it is made, and a new one is zeroed in full ([New textures start as zero](rendering.md#new-textures-start-as-zero)), whatever part of it the game ever writes. Ocarina of Time makes a 12288x12288 Depth16Unorm (288 MiB) and a 4096x4096 Depth32Float (64 MiB) at each save load, and in the areas looked at only samples the first, which holds the zeros it was made with ([the entry above](#a-clear-pass-on-a-large-depth-texture-fails-with-insufficient-memory)). Making it smaller than the game asked is not safe in general: a shader that reads it with integer coordinates the game computed from its own size would read past it, and the host cannot see such reads (shaders reach textures through bindless pools, and the reflection records no texel fetch).
- **Fix:** on by default on the iPhone and iPad for the X2.0 test round, opt-in on the Mac: `DM_NVN_DEPTH_LAZY=1` (profile key `depth_lazy`, which no game sets yet; `=0` turns it off). A new single-sample, single-level 2D Depth16Unorm or Depth32Float texture of 64 MB or more (`LZ_MIN`), outside a sparse pool and a resolution family, is made a Metal placement sparse texture at its full size with no tile mapped (`nvn_mtl.m` `lz_make`). An unmapped tile reads 0 to every read, `read()` at any integer coordinate, plain and comparison samples and copies out of it included, and drops what is written to it: what a newly zeroed texture holds, so it is not zeroed either. A 64 KB tile (256x128 for Depth16, 128x128 for Depth32F) is mapped from the sparse tiles' placement heaps (`sp_slot`, 64 MB each) and zeroed by a copy in the upload batch only where the GPU is about to write something other than 0 (`lz_map`): the mapping goes to the queue first (`rec_queue_block`), and the batch is committed before the command buffer that writes (`cb_seal`). What maps: a draw that writes depth (test and write on, no rasterizer discard), the tiles under its scissor and its viewport's box with 256 pixels round it for wide points and lines (`lz_draw`); a scissored clear to another depth than 0, the scissor's tiles (`clear_ds_tri`); and every tile for what the host cannot bound: a texture copy or resolve into it, a buffer-to-texture copy, a texture clear through a colour view, an image binding, an attachment through a colour view. A texture clear of a part of it or of a channel mask in its own format maps nothing: the host draws none for a depth format (red line 44), and it mapped and zeroed every tile for it before. A clear to 0 maps nothing, a texture clear of the whole level included. A whole clear to another depth, through a load action (NVN clears the whole target) or a texture clear of the whole level, would map every tile for good, so it makes the texture a normal one first (`lz_whole`): made as without the switch (a recycled heap or an allocation of its own, lossless compression kept), but not zeroed, since the clear writes every texel before anything reads it. That only while it adds at most an eighth more memory than mapping the other tiles would (`lz_whole_pays`; an eighth is a recycled heap's headroom for a target up to 32 MB): the placement heaps are never freed, so the tiles the texture has mapped would stay in them, free, and tiles already free there map the rest with no new memory. Mapping is counted as the free tiles and whole 64 MB heaps for what they lack, the normal texture as its size. So a texture every tile of which a draw mapped first stays lazy (a normal one would add 64 MB to its 64 MB of tiles), as does one the heaps' free tiles can hold, and every tile is mapped as before; Ocarina of Time's save load, with no tile mapped and none free, gets the normal texture. The normal texture takes every name of the lazy one (the serials that hold it, live and finalized, their pins and the sharing table: `tex_names_move`, which `tex_upgrade` shares) and its render-target release record; each serial is noted (`ser_note`), so only that texture's IDs and pool table entries are taken again. The lazy one is retired once the submissions that used it complete. Tiles stay mapped while the texture lives and go back to the heaps' free list after the GPU is done with it. A lazy texture, or the normal one a whole clear made of it, goes with its memory pool's Finalize as every texture of the pool does ([A texture object finalized after its pool kept the pool's memory](#a-texture-object-finalized-after-its-pool-kept-the-pools-memory)); one a pin still holds after that is never made a normal texture nor mapped by a whole clear (`lz_whole`), which would make its whole size again under a serial the Finalize ended, kept until the texture object's own Finalize. Everything else sees the full-size texture: sizes, views, viewports, copies. It is never remade for a usage (`tex_upgrade` keeps a placement sparse texture, and it has every usage a view or clear could ask for), the background purge leaves sparse textures alone, and the memory census counts its mapped tiles under the placement heaps, not the texture (a lazy texture's own allocation is 8-64 KB). A GPU without placement sparse depth textures (`sparseTileSizeWithTextureType` gives no 64 KB tile) makes the texture as before, with one line.
- **Log:** `lazy depth: single-sample 2D Depth16 / Depth32F textures of 64 MB or more take memory only where the GPU writes them (placement sparse) (DM_NVN_DEPTH_LAZY)` at the first look; for each texture `lazy depth texture ser=S 12288x12288 Depth16Unorm: 288 MB not backed until the GPU writes it (placement sparse, 4608 tiles of 256x128, none mapped; …)`, then `lazy depth texture ser=S …: N of M tiles mapped (X of Y MB) by a draw / a scissored clear / a texture copy … in frame F (made in frame G)` at its first mapping and as it reaches each quarter; for a whole clear to another depth, `lazy depth texture ser=S 12288x12288 Depth16Unorm: made a normal texture by a clear to a non-zero depth in frame F (made in frame G, N of M tiles mapped): every tile would be mapped, so it is backed whole (288 MB) and not zeroed, the clear writing every texel` (or `by a texture clear to a non-zero depth`; the first 64), and where it stays lazy instead, `lazy depth texture ser=S 4096x4096 Depth32Float: a clear to a non-zero depth maps its other N tiles in frame F instead of making it a normal texture (M of 1024 mapped, K free in the placement heaps): the heaps, which are never freed, grow by X MB, a normal texture would take 64 MB` (the first 64); every 3600 frames while the mapped tiles change, and at exit, `lazy depth (every 3600 frames): K textures live of N made (W made normal textures by a whole clear to a non-zero depth, V mapped whole there instead), D MB declared, T tiles mapped (X MB), U MB not backed; at most P tiles mapped at once (… MB)`. A texture that never logs a mapping was only sampled; one that maps every tile soon after it is made (a copy into it, say) saves nothing and costs the mapping and zeroing of its tiles.
- **Where it does not help:** a texture the game clears whole to another depth than 0 (it becomes a normal texture at that clear: the memory it takes with the switch off, less the zeroing; or, where its tiles and the heaps' free ones hold most of it, every tile mapped) or copies into (every tile mapped, as before plus the mapping); a texture in a resolution family (`image_texture` takes it first) or a sparse pool (the game's own mappings, `sparse_gpu_make`). The first draw into a region maps and zeroes its tiles in that frame's upload batch: a shadow pass over the whole 12288x12288 would map 4,608 tiles at once. Mapped tiles come in 64 MB heaps that are never freed: a texture the game frees keeps no memory of its own, but a heap made for its tiles stays, free for the next lazy or sparse texture (Star Fox, below: 64 MB after its Depth16 went).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_depth_lazy`, under the Metal validation layer with the encode thread off and on. Every case runs the same operations on a lazy texture and on the texture the host makes without the switch, and every texel's `read()`, plain sample and comparison sample must match. A new 4096x4096 Depth32F and a 12288x12288 Depth16 hold no texel memory and read 0 (integer reads far into the texture, samples, comparison samples, a copy out); draws through `lz_draw` map only the tiles under a scissor, under a flipped viewport's box and its margin, and none for a draw without depth writes; tiles that come back from a freed lazy texture that held 0.75 read 0 when mapped again (no second heap made); production clears through the replay: a whole one to 0 maps nothing, a scissored one to 0.6 maps its tiles, a whole one to 1 makes it a normal texture with no tile more mapped, holding what the plain texture holds, which the serial, a second texture over the same storage, the sharing table and a texture ID (both serials noted) all resolve to, and a draw into it maps nothing (the heaps' free tiles taken first, as another sparse texture would take them); the lazy one leaves the residency set once the GPU is done with it; a whole clear to 1 of a texture every tile of which a draw mapped keeps it lazy, no tile more mapped and no heap made, and so does one of a texture with no tile mapped while a freed lazy texture's tiles are free in the heaps, which map all of it (no heap made), both reading 1; a texture clear of the whole level to 0 maps nothing, nor do ones of a part, of a channel mask and of a part through a Depth32F view (no clear drawn), and one to 0.4 makes it a normal texture; Ocarina of Time's save load, a new 12288x12288 Depth16 and a 4096x4096 Depth32F whose first use is a whole clear to 1 (made lazy by the clear's attachment, then normal): no tile mapped, no placement heap made, both read 1, and both lazy ones gone once the GPU is done; a pool finalized before its texture objects takes a lazy texture with tiles mapped (its tiles back to the heaps once the GPU is done) and a normal one a whole clear made, and a pinned lazy one cleared whole to 1 after that is neither made a normal texture nor mapped and gives its serial no entry (2 checks fail without `lz_whole`'s check); a texture copy maps every tile and holds what the plain texture holds; the switch off, the profile's default, sizes under 64 MB and depth-stencil formats keep the old path, and a GPU without placement sparse depth textures (`g_lz_test_nosparse`) gets the old texture, zeroed. The counts of lazy textures and tiles are read once what the GPU is done with has been released (`settle`: the encode thread, the feedback handlers, the residency commits they hand on and the drains they kick off the submit thread, `defer_kick_group`, whose commit drops the last reference on a utility-QoS thread); read right after `mtl_drain`, they met a lazy texture such a commit freed later, which failed the pool case up to 1 run in 10 under load and the save-load and freed-tile cases about 1 in 150 (those since before the pool case). The pinned texture's lookups have a pool of their own: with ARC's return handshake off, as under ThreadSanitizer, the outer pool kept it to the end. The draws' GPU half is the test's own pass: the host's draw replay calls the same `lz_draw`, but no game has run with the switch.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (its save load's two depth textures; the owner's iPhone 18 Pro Max logs, X1.0). The X2.0 test round on that phone (build 431d5d18, on by default): Ocarina of Time's save load clears both to a non-zero depth as it makes them, so all 5,632 tiles were mapped and zeroed in that frame (its GPU time 159 ms, then frames of 600 and 649 ms) and nothing was saved; the round turned the switch off for it in `Documents/env.txt` until the whole clear made a normal texture, which is not yet run on the phone. [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (the same round's run 1): an 8192x8192 Depth16 had 512 of its 2,048 tiles (32 of 128 MB) mapped by draws in the frame it was made and no more until the game freed it, between frames 3,600 and 7,200 (the totals: 2,560 tiles mapped at frame 3,600, the most at once, then 2,048 with one texture live); those tiles took a third 64 MB placement heap made for them, so it held 64 MB where the texture backed whole holds 128 while it lived, and the heap stays after it (placement heaps are never freed). An 8192x4096 Depth32F was mapped whole by a texture copy in the frame it was made and saved nothing. Other games with large depth or shadow targets take it the same way: the log has a `lazy depth texture` line for each texture it took.

### iOS's memory-kill reports go into the log

- **What:** when iOS ends a run for going over the app's memory limit, iOS 27's MetricKit hands a later start its report (usually within a day). `dm/ios/Sources/MemoryReports.swift` (started in `main.m` before `UIApplicationMain`) writes a `[memory]` line into log.txt (the time range, the `com.nx18.screen` / `com.nx18.hold` states at the time, Low Power Mode) and the full report, with call stacks, to `Documents/logs/memory-kill-<time>.json`. Nothing leaves the device.
- **Hangs and crashes too (2026-11-21):** the same stream's hang reports (the main thread unresponsive, with the system's call stacks of every thread, which is what the open iPhone freeze needs) and crash reports are kept the same way: a `[hang]` line with the hang's length, or a `[crash]` line, in log.txt and the full report as `Documents/logs/hang-<time>.json` / `crash-<time>.json`. Idea from Apple's sample "Track performance by app state using MetricKit". Nothing leaves the device.
- **What it does not say:** the limit itself. The phone's own `JetsamEvent-…` files (Settings › Privacy & Security › Analytics & Improvements › Analytics Data) do.
